## 8. Complete card catalog

All 268 cards of the three shipped sets — Core's 100 ([[§8.1]]–[[§8.5]]), Classic's 90 ([[§8.6]]) and Classic+'s 78 ([[§8.7]]) — plus the tokens a card defines (Core's 5, index N.1, and Classic+'s 28, index N.k, each after its card) and the ten AI generated cards ([[§8.7]]'s end), and the Meditative set's 99 cards and 30 tokens ([[§8.8]]), which are in the catalog before they ship ([[R1420]]) and join `catalog.json` part by part as issue #496 builds them, all in index order (Core's 4 shared tokens, The Coin and the Ghoul Token are in section 7), described by what they do to the game state rather than by their printed text; stats read base → radiant, and the Engine column names the primitive from section 6 or the ruling from section 11 that the implementation depends on. Every Radiant face meets [[R275]]'s standard, and `docs/radiant-audit.md` records each card against it. The catalog prints each Radiant face in full: its `radiant.text` is the Radiant cell read by the Conventions below and written out ([[R277]]). A card's `refs` list the cards and tokens its text names ([[R279]]).

Conventions: "target" means the player picks at play time from all legal units and heroes on either side unless narrowed. "Your" means the controller. A Cry or spell whose target set is empty fizzles: the unit still enters, the spell still counts as played. Damage always goes through the pipeline in section 4.4; "lose health" does not. Reading a Radiant cell: a cell that lists keywords without "Plus" gives the radiant form's complete keyword list; "Plus X" adds keyword X to the base keywords; a cell that names no keywords keeps the base keywords. A clause the cell restates replaces the base version and every base clause it does not restate is kept; a cell that changes only a number ("+10 attack", "5 damage") changes only that number. "same" says so explicitly, and "Also" and "Then" add effects.

Core's rarity is assigned by mechanical complexity and game impact, and is the value the catalog's `rarity` field carries. Common: keywords only, one primitive with at most one target, or one fixed start-of-turn, end-of-turn or cast-on-draw effect. Rare: a trigger that reads state or asks for a choice, a modal choice or prompt, a cost or mana modifier, or a simple trap. Epic: chained prompts, delayed or cross-turn effects, traps that act on the opponent's turn, or effects that read several zones. Legendary: control changes across the board, zone-wide rewrites, recursion, or a stat layer that exists for one card. Mythic: a subsystem of its own. Distribution: 32 Common, 40 Rare, 16 Epic, 7 Legendary, 5 Mythic (patch v0.2.9, issue #44, made #27, #28 and #40 Rare). This supersedes the rarities printed in the source list (#1–20 Common, #21–50 Rare, #51–80 Epic, #81–95 Legendary, #96–100 Mythic); a review comparing catalog to source should treat that difference as intended. Classic and Classic+ keep the designer's rarities: their source list gives rarity card by card, so each is a choice rather than an index block, and rarity feeds pools (a random Legendary, a random Book). Classic: 35 Common, 26 Rare, 18 Epic, 10 Legendary, 1 Mythic (90). Classic+: 13 Common, 24 Rare, 25 Epic, 13 Legendary, 3 Mythic (78). A Classic+ token carries `rarity: "Token"` like every token, so no pool ever finds one by rarity, and the rarity the designer printed on it as `printedRarity` ([[§5]]), which the Rarity column shows in parentheses: Legendary on the eight Pancakes, the five Losers, KY's Gift, Felinor Flagbearer Prime, Classic Golem and the J-lease Pack; Epic on Otherworldly Removal's three and Solarius Prime; Rare on Bone Storm and Brother Ping; Common, Common, Rare, Legendary and Mythic on the five Grapes; none on the AI generated cards.

Rarity measures complexity and how much a card can swing a game, not raw stats; power is tuned with numbers and Radiant. Common is one idea read in one pass: vanilla or keyword Units, or a single effect (damage, draw, heal, destroy, bounce, counter); it can touch one card in another zone or reset the board in one line, with no build-arounds and no rule changes. Rare is one idea with a twist: conditions, Choose one, cost changes, making cards Radiant, stealing a single card; small engines that repeat a simple effect; a theme rewarded that still works in any deck. Epic is engines and payoffs a deck is built around; repeated generation of a random card or token every turn; mass effects on the opponent's hand or deck; multi-part effects and set showcases. Legendary takes over, swaps or replaces a whole resource (deck, hand, board or Hero health); breaks a core rule for one player; named characters and effects a game is remembered by. Mythic breaks the frame of the game: sets rules for the whole match, persists across games, hands a turn to an AI, or could not exist on a normal card. Distribution targets: Common 20-40%, Rare 25-30%, Epic 20-30%, Legendary 10-15%, Mythic 5% or less.

Families share one rarity: 2+ cards with the same naming template, card type and effect shape, so one card can be guessed from another. The family's rarity wins over the criteria; a member too strong or weak for it changes numbers, not rarity (Book of Vital Kill stays Epic although setting Hero health is Legendary territory).

| Family | Rarity |
| --- | --- |
| Book of ___ | Epic |
| Call to Chaos (___ Edition) | Legendary |
| ___ Glowy Jelly Bean | Rare |
| The Power to ___ | Rare |
| ___ in the System | Epic |
| ___ of the Forgotten (Echoes, Curse) | Rare |
| Rapid ___ (Replenish, Draw) | Common |
| ___ Shroom | Epic |
| Plague Bringer (and Goliath) | Rare |
| Jlockeed ___ | Common |
| Right-house defender / Wrong-House Attacker | Common |

Tags are not families: Human, Felinor, KY, CN, Fruit and Plague cards take any rarity, and Call to Arms is no Call to Chaos, the templates differing. Tokens take the rarity of the card that makes them; generic tokens many cards make (Rush, Sheep, Felinor, Ghoul) have no rarity. Real cards made by other cards keep their own rarity (Book of Flame from Devil's Pact, Reminisce from Field of Dreams). A bigger version of the same effect is never a lower rarity than the smaller one (Call to Arms Recruits at Common, so Pile On's Recruit of the deck is Epic). Radiant never changes rarity. One name names one card ([[R739]]).

A Core row whose card declares numbers (`params`, [[R386]]) ends its Engine cell with "Tunes:", read as [[§8.6]]'s rows read it; a Core row without one declares none (patch v0.3.2, issue #493).

### 8.6 Classic

Ninety cards, `classic-001` to `classic-090`, from the designer's list of 2026-09-30 (patch v0.2.0, [[R380]] on). Classic generates only cards that exist elsewhere (the Felinor Token, C #16 Book of Flame, C #34 Ancient Acquisition), so it defines no token. The Engine column ends with "Tunes:", the numbers Degrade, Upgrade and C+ #41 KY's Constant may change (`params`, [[§5]], [[R386]]; a numbered keyword's number, such as C+ #19.1's Armor 3 or C+ #38's Spell Damage +2, is listed too, though the catalog keeps it on the keyword, where [[R386]]'s X change moves it, and not in `params`): ↑ means more is better for the card's controller, ↓ less; a step is 1 for a number up to 5, 2 for 6 to 12 and a quarter (rounded) above that, read off the base face's value (one step serves both faces), unless the cell names one; an amount never drops below 1, and a threshold moves toward harder for a Degrade.

| # | Name | Rarity | Cost | Type, tags | Stats | Base effect | Radiant effect | Engine |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Curse of the Forgotten Classic | Rare | 1 | Spell |  | Deal 1 damage to the enemy hero for each card in their exile; draw 1 | Also Recruit a card from their exile; if it is a Unit, it attacks the enemy hero at once | One hit of N on the enemy hero, N = the opponent's exile size as it resolves: "for each card" in one sentence is one hit, so Armor applies once, as Hearthstone reads it, and N = 0 is no hit ([[R63]]); then the draw. The Radiant face keeps the draw ("Also" adds to the base face, and [[R275]] wants it stronger). Recruit ([[§6.3]]) from the opponent's exile: exile is chronological ([[§3]]), so Recruit's top-down scan is newest first, and the most recently exiled permanent card there is summoned under your control, no Cry ([[R1]]), its owner unchanged, so it goes to its owner's piles when it leaves the field ([[§3.2]]); a Unit then makes one forced attack on the enemy hero ([[R53]]), summoning sickness ignored; with no permanent in their exile, or no open zone for the newest one, nothing is recruited (never an older card), and a recruited Trap is set face-down, read by you alone ([[R33]]). A `preview` ([[R280]]) shows N. Tunes: damage per card 1 ↑; draw 1 ↑. |
| 2 | The Trickster | Common | 1 | Unit, Human | 2/1 → 4/2 | Cry: your next Trap or Field Spell costs (2) less | Cry: your next Trap or Field Spell costs (0) | A price rule on its controller that lasts until used (`addCostRule`; Cost, [[§6.3]], [[R65]], [[R455]]) for Traps, Field Traps and Field Spells, spent by the next such card you play, even one that already costs (0), and never by a cast ([[R70]]); "next" has no "this turn", so it waits across turns until used; Field Trap counts as Trap. The Radiant face sets that card's cost to (0) instead of discounting it. Tunes: discount 2 ↑, on the base face only; the Radiant face prints no number and is not tuned ([[R1431]]); Radiant cost 0 ↓. |
| 3 | Book of Heal | Epic | 1 | Spell, Book |  | Heal a target 9 | Heal 18 | [[§6.3]] Heal on any unit or hero, as #47 Fig of Life reads "Heal" ([[R19]]). Tunes: heal 9 ↑ (step 2). |
| 4 | Palantir | Legendary | 1 | Field Spell, Jlockheed |  | Aura: your opponent can't draw more than 1 card each turn. When your opponent plays a Book, Tribute this to steal it | Same Aura; when your opponent plays a Spell, steal it, with no Tribute; once the Spells this has stolen total (2) Cost or more, Tribute this | A draw limit ([[§2.4]], [[R457]]) on the opponent, on every turn: draws past it do not happen at all. The steal answers the opponent's announce of a Spell with the Book tag as a Counter ([[§6.3]]), in [[§10.5]]'s announce window (step 3a): the base face steals with no "you may" (balance patch 1, mandatory), Tributing itself at once, after the traps the announce woke ([[R448]]); the Book is countered and moves to your hand as yours (Steal off the field, [[§6.3]] Steal; the owner changes, [[§3.2]], [[R12]], [[R466]]; the hand cap applies, and a burned card goes to its new owner's graveyard). "Tribute this to steal it" is a price paid without asking: no prompt opens and the Book never resolves while Palantir stands. The Radiant face steals every Spell with no question, adds each one's own cost ([[R65]], out of play; an X Spell 0, [[R396]]) to `memory.stolenCost` (a Spell a trap has already countered is neither stolen nor counted, [[R448]]), and sacrifices itself once that reaches (2). A cast by an effect is a play ([[R70]]) and is answered too. Tributing this works where it stands. Tunes: draw limit 1 ↓ (never below 1); Radiant threshold 2 ↑. |
| 5 | Tesla | Epic | 2 | Field Trap | 1/4 → 2/8 | Animated, Lifesteal; when your opponent summons a Unit: deal 4 damage to it, then summon this as a Unit in Defense Position | 8 damage | A Field Trap answering each Unit that arrives on the opponent's side, however it is summoned ([[§6.3]] Summon: played, cast, token, Recruit, Reborn); a card already on the field that crosses to that side by a steal ([[R171]]) or steps into a unit zone by animating ([[R383]], [[R445]]) was not summoned and does not set it off; a played Unit is answered after it resolves, as #60 Bear Honeypot answers ([[R17]], Hearthstone's Snipe), so its Cry happens first. The hit's source is Tesla, whose Lifesteal heals you ([[§4.4]] step 8). Then it animates in Defense Position (Animated, [[§6.1]], [[R383]]), into the unit zone in its lane when open, else the leftmost open, unlocked, unreserved one ([[R64]]); already a Unit, it stays put. It keeps firing while animated, a turret, and is never consumed; with no open unit zone it stays face-up in its backrow zone and fires from there. Face-down it is hidden like any trap until it first fires ([[R33]]). Tunes: damage 4 ↑. |

Activate: cast a copy of that Spell | Cry: exile a (2) Cost or less Spell from your hand; same Activate | The Cry (a Field Spell's Cry, as #73 Anti-oneshot Armor's) declares a hand pick ([[R81]]) filtered to Spells that cost (1) or less (Radiant (2)) per [[R65]], exiles it and remembers it (`memory.scepter = { defId, radiant }`); the Radiant face keeps "from your hand", since the Cry has nowhere else to look; with no such Spell the Cry fizzles. Activate ([[§6.2]], [[R384]], once per turn): Cast ([[§6.3]]) a fresh copy of the remembered Spell, its radiant flag kept, its choices made by you in prompts as the cast begins ([[R70]], [[R81]]), the `activate` action carrying none ([[R453]]); a held X-cost Spell (it costs (0) in hand, so the Cry may exile it) is cast with the X you choose then, 1 to your current mana, unpaid ([[R520]]); a card cast from no zone goes to your graveyard after it resolves ([[R87]]). With nothing remembered the ability can't be activated. The exiled card stays in exile. Leaving the field clears the memory ([[R78]]). Tunes: cost limit 1 ↑. |
| 8 | Pickle | Rare | 1 | Spell |  | Your opponent chooses three times: they discard a card, they exile the bottom card of their deck, or you draw a card | Your opponent chooses three times: they discard 2 cards, they exile the bottom 2 cards of their deck, or you draw 2 cards | Three mode prompts held by the opponent ([[§10.6]]), one after another, repeats allowed; "discard" discards at random from their hand ([[R682]]), no prompt. Only choices that would do something are offered, and "you draw" always is. The opponent answers during your turn, a non-active player's prompt on its own clock ([[R79]]); a timeout answers with the AI policy. Tunes: choices 3 ↑; discard 1 ↑; exile 1 ↑; draw 1 ↑. |
| 9 | Income Tax | Legendary | 2 | Trap |  | When your opponent draws their 2nd card in a turn: they keep one card of their choice and give you the rest of their hand | Also the cards you get cost (1) less | The opponent's draws this turn as [[R521]] counts them ([[§10.1]], [[R457]]), on either player's turn: it fires on the draw that brings the count to exactly 2 when that draw draws a card, so on their own turn, the start-of-turn draw being the first, any extra draw sets it off. The trap fires once that draw is complete (a cast-on-draw card is cast first, [[R58]]). The opponent picks the one hand card to keep (a `hand` prompt they hold, its options read by them alone, [[R177]]); the rest move to your hand and become yours (cards between players' piles, [[§6.3]] Steal, [[§3.2]]; the owner changes, [[R12]], [[R466]]); your hand cap burns the overflow into your graveyard. A hand of one card or none gives nothing and asks nothing. Radiant: `costMod` −1 on each card you get. Tunes: trigger draw 2 ↓ (never below 2); Radiant discount 1 ↑. |
| 10 | Exile | Common | 1 | Trap |  | When your opponent plays a card that costs (2) or less: Counter it and exile it | When your opponent plays a card that costs (3) or less: Counter it and exile it; then exile random enemy permanents whose costs total at most (3) minus its cost | Counter ([[§6.3]], [[R448]]), in [[§10.5]]'s announce window, the countered card exiled rather than sent to the graveyard. "Costs" is the cost paid, as #60 Bear Honeypot reads it ([[R56]]), so a card cast for free ([[R70]]) always qualifies. Radiant: a budget of the threshold ((3) on the Radiant face, tuned with it) minus the countered card's cost paid, as the designer's "until the difference in cost is made up" reads; repeat: pick a random enemy permanent (the top of a unit pile or a backrow card, face-down ones included, [[R13]]) whose cost ([[R65]] as it stands on the field; an X card at the X it was played for, 0 with none chosen, [[R396]]) is at most the budget, exile it, subtract its cost; stop when the budget is 0 or nothing fits; a (0) Cost permanent always fits while the budget is above 0. The name is also a rules word, which the reference proof never reads as this card unless `refs` lists it ([[R381]]). Tunes: threshold 2 ↑ (Radiant 3). |
| 11 | Mind Melt | Common | 1 | Spell |  | Look at your opponent's hand; exile a card from it | Look at your opponent's hand; choose a cost; exile every card of that cost from it | Look at a hand ([[§6.3]], [[§10.8]]): their hand cards are the options of a `hand` prompt of one card ([[§10.6]]), seen by you alone. | 90 | In Too Deep | Mythic | 1 | Field Spell, Quickdraw |  | Indestructible; each quest you complete offers rewards, and the reward you choose sets your next quest (quest 1's text lives in the quest line, not the card text: it appears on the card face only after the card is played) | Each quest you complete gives every reward it offers, and you follow every path | Quests ([[§10.1]], [[R404]]): `memory.quest = { active, progress, done, auras }`, the tree data in the card file. Quest 1 opens as the card enters the field. Each quest, what completes it (counted from the moment it opens), and its rewards → next quest: 1, you have drawn 2 cards: A → 2, B → 3; 2, 2 enemy permanents have been destroyed by anything: C → 4, D → 5; 3, you control 3 or more permanents at once: D → 5, E → 6; 4, you end a turn with 3 or more unspent mana: F → 7, G → 8; 5, your cards have dealt 12 damage to enemies in total: G → 8, H → 9; 6, your Units have 10 or more total attack and 10 or more total health at once: H → 9, I → 10; 7, you end a turn with 5 or more unspent mana: J, the end of the line; 8, 3 cards have entered either exile pile: K, the end; 9, a draw of yours takes the last card of your deck (a deck already empty when the quest opens completes it at once): L, the end; 10, your graveyard holds 6 or more Units: M, the end. Rewards: A, heal your hero 6; B, deal 3 damage to a target; C, return 2 random cards from your graveyard to your hand; D, place 3 Plague Counters ([[§6.3]], [[R689]]); E, a random Unit of yours gets +3/+3; F, bounce target permanent; G, your opponent discards 2 cards of their choice ([[R16]]); H, draw 2; I, Recruit a card; J, gain 100 mana; K, exile your opponent's deck; L, Aura: you may play cards from your graveyard (Play from the graveyard, [[§6.3]] Play); M, Aura: your Units have Indestructible. Completion is noticed at the state check after the event that completes it, on either player's turn, and the reward choice is a `reward` prompt ([[§10.6]]) for the card's controller then (a non-active player's prompt on the opponent's turn). The auras L and M hold while In Too Deep is on the field; it is Indestructible, so usually for the rest of the game, but a Tribute or an exile ends them, and leaving the field resets the quest line ([[R78]]). The view shows the open quest or quests with their progress ("1/2") and the rewards on offer; the collection prints the whole tree. Radiant: each completed quest grants all of its rewards and opens all of their quests, with no reward prompt; a quest reached by two paths opens once, and a reward two completed quests offer (D, G, H) is granted by each. The conditions read the designer's quests as countable ([[R404]]): "Destroy 2 cards" counts enemy permanents destroyed by anything, "Deal 12 damage" counts damage your cards deal to enemies, "Exile 3 cards" counts cards entering either exile, "Float" is unspent mana at the end of your turn, and "Draw your entire Deck" is done by the draw that empties it, never by cards leaving it any other way. Quickdraw: it starts in your opening hand ([[§2.1]]). Tunes: none. |

### 8.7 Classic+

Seventy-eight cards, `classicplus-001` to `classicplus-078`, from the designer's list of 2026-09-30 (patch v0.2.0, [[R380]] on), each token a card defines right after it (index N.k, id `classicplus-NNN-k`), then the ten AI generated cards (T-AI-1 to T-AI-10, `classicplus-t-ai-01` to `-10`), which C+ #43 AI Slop and C+ #78 Claude's Datacenter hand out. The designer used five numbers twice and numbered two tokens as cards (one of them under a doubled number); the first card listed under a number keeps it, and the others are renumbered: Jogg's Box #47, Brother Lar #76, Brother Ping #76.1, Anti-Softlock #77, Claude's Datacenter #78, Felinor Flagbearer Prime #46.1 and the J-lease J-Jungle EX-plorer Pack #75.1 ([[§5.3]]). "Tunes:" is as in [[§8.6]].

The Radiant face leaves you one main-phase action (a play, an attack, a position switch or an activation; ending the turn yourself also uses it), and the turn ends once that action resolves ([[R415]]). Tunes: Radiant actions 1 ↑. |

| 35 | Rollback | Legendary | 4 | Spell |  | Choose 1, 2 or 3. Return the board to how it was that many turns ago | Choose 1, 2 or 3, and your side, your opponent's side or both. Return that part of the board to how it was that many turns ago | Board snapshots ([[R419]]): at the start of every turn, before the mana refresh, the state records the field (both sides' unit piles and backrow zones as full instance data, dormant cards included, and each side's Locks) and keeps the last four (`state.boardHistory`: this turn's and the three before). "N turns ago" is the snapshot taken at the start of the player-turn N before this one. N, and on the Radiant face the side, are declared at play ([[R81]]). Restoring a side takes three steps, each done on every restored side before the next: 1. each card on that side now that the snapshot doesn't hold leaves the field for its controller's hand (a Bounce, [[R747]]; [[R78]]'s reset; a full hand burns it, [[§2.4]]; a unit token ceases to exist, [[R11]]); 2. each card the snapshot holds is put back in its zone and place in its pile with the snapshot's instance data (damage, buffs, granted keywords, position, counters, memory, radiant flag, face-down or face-up, controller, and its definition, so a card transformed or fused since is restored as it was), moved from wherever it is now (a hand, a deck, a graveyard, exile, the other side) or recreated from the snapshot if it has ceased to exist (a token, a card transformed or fused away), and a card that goes back face-down into the backrow from anywhere else takes a fresh id as it goes ([[R227]]); 3. that side's Locks become the snapshot's. Nothing else changes: health, mana, decks and graveyards stay, bar the cards that moved, and any home-zone reservation of a moved card is cleared. The moves are not plays, summons or deaths, so no Cry and no Death: one `rolledBack` event names N and the sides, followed by each moved card's own event, hidden per zone ([[§10.8]]). `viewFor` never sends the history. Tunes: none. |

| 42 | KY's Test | Legendary | 1 | Spell, KY |  | Offer an easy, a medium, and a hard problem, each with a random reward. | Same, each with a random Radiant reward. | The question bank ([[R420]]). As the Spell resolves, one reward is rolled per difficulty (an entry of its list, uniformly; Hard has one), then the three difficulties are offered in a Discover ([[§6.3]]), each labelled with its rolled reward. Then an `answer` prompt, the multiple choice: a problem of the chosen difficulty (match rng), its statement and its four options in an rng-shuffled order; the answer is one option. The bank is data in `crates/cards`, public like the catalog, versioned with it ([[R388]]): entries `{ id, difficulty, statement, options (4), answer }`, at least 30 Medium and 30 Hard (`KY_TEST_MIN_PROBLEMS`); Easy problems are generated from the rng instead: a + b with a and b drawn from 10 to 99 (`KY_TEST_EASY_ADDENDS`) and three different wrong sums, each a + b moved by one of ±1, ±2, ±10 (`KY_TEST_EASY_MISSES`). Statements are plain text with Unicode maths (∫, ², √, subscripts), so a client needs no maths renderer. The engine checks the answer and the key never leaves it: it stays in the prompt's resume data (problem id or generated addends, option order, answer index), which `viewFor` never sends ([[§10.8]]); the chooser sees the reward labels, the statement and the options, the opponent only that a prompt is open ([[§10.6]]) and then the reward's ordinary events. A right answer gains the reward shown on that difficulty; a wrong one gives nothing, and the Spell was still played. Rewards: pools of non-token cards of every set ([[R380]]) but KY's Test ([[R387]]), repeats allowed ([[R60]]), the hand cap burning extras ([[§2.4]]); 3 The Coins are three Core The Coin tokens ([[§7]]), which need no pool ([[R244]]); the (2) and (4) Cost cards are priced per [[R65]]; the Legendary is any non-token Legendary set to (0) (`costOverride`), as the Medium (4) Cost cards are set to (1); "fill your hand" adds random Books until the hand holds 10; KY's Gift is C+ #42.1 with `costOverride` 0. Radiant: every reward card is Radiant. The turn clock keeps running while a problem is open, as for every prompt ([[R79]]). The bank ships in the client bundle, so a determined player can look answers up; keeping the keys server-only would cost hotseat and practice the card. Tunes: none. |

| 62 | KY's Papaya | Epic | 1 | Spell, Fruit, KY |  | Draw a curve y = ax³ + bx² + cx + d across the board, where x is the lane (0 to 4 from your lane 1) and y the row (0 your backrow, 1 your units, 2 their units, 3 their backrow); exile every card on the curve | Exile every enemy card on the curve | Curve targeting ([[R422]]). The board is a grid of 20 cells from the caster's seat: x = lane − 1 on both sides ([[§3.1]] faces lane N with lane N), y = 0 the caster's backrow, 1 the caster's unit row, 2 the opponent's unit row, 3 the opponent's backrow. The player picks points, not coefficients: as the Spell resolves, board-cell prompts (`cell`, [[§10.6]]) ask for one cell at a time, each offering the 4 cells of every lane not yet used (empty, occupied or Locked alike) and, after the first, "done"; a fourth cell ends the picking (`PAPAYA_MAX_CELLS`). Each prompt so has at most 21 answers, under `MAX_PROMPT_ANSWERS` (256), and every curve stays reachable by `legalActions`, the fuzz suite and the AI, which one prompt of all ≈2,100 cell sets would not allow. The curve is the lowest-degree polynomial through the chosen cells (Lagrange interpolation in exact rationals, degree at most 3: one cell a constant, two a line, three a parabola, four a cubic). It is read at every lane, x = 0 to 4: a zone is on the curve when its cell satisfies y = p(x) exactly, so the curve also meets cells in lanes nobody picked (one cell's constant crosses its whole row, and a line through two cells may meet a third); between lanes it touches nothing. The card at each such cell is exiled: the top of each pile, face-down cards included, a card dormant beneath resuming ([[§3.2]]). The Radiant exiles only the enemy's (rows 2 and 3). A client may show the equation the chosen cells make. The opponent sees that a prompt is open, then the exiles. Tunes: none. |

| 74 | Twice Forward One Step Backwards | Mythic | 2 | Field Trap |  | Brittle 2; every second card your opponent plays, fuse the last into this and gain +1 Brittle | Brittle 4; every second card your opponent plays, fuse a Radiant copy of the last into this and gain +1 Brittle | A Field Trap ([[R425]]): a Trap is consumed when it fires, which would leave nothing to gain Brittle. It has no Brittle while it is face-down ([[R687]]): its printed Brittle starts when it first activates and turns face-up (Brittle, [[§6.1]], [[R385]]), then ticks at its controller's starts of turn, and at 0 it is destroyed (Indestructible ignores that); destroyed while still face-down, it is shown to the opponent as it goes. It counts the opponent's plays since it was set (`memory.plays`; casts count, [[R70]]; a countered card was never played). On each even count, once that card has resolved ([[§10.5]] step 7), the card, if it still exists (a Unit on the field, a Spell in the graveyard, a trap in the backrow), is fused into this (Fuse, [[§6.3]], [[R77]], [[R102]]): this is the kept instance and stays a Field Trap, and the opponent's card ceases to exist. The Radiant fuses in a Radiant copy of the card's definition instead and leaves the card where it is. Either way this then gains +1 Brittle, on every even count. The texts fused in work for you where they can: a fused Unit's end-of-turn line runs on your side, a fused aura covers your side; a fused Cry never runs, since this is already on the field. It turns face-up, public, the first time it activates, whether or not anything is left to fuse ([[R33]], [[R687]]); until then its Brittle count and its play count are read by its controller only ([[§6.1]]). Tunes: Brittle 2 ↑ (its X); every 2 ↓ (never below 2); Brittle gained 1 ↑. |

The damage is one instance per hero, 1 (Radiant 2) times the count, through [[§4.4]], so Spell Damage raises it once ([[§4.4]] step 0); none destroyed, no damage. Its preview ([[R280]]) is the damage each hero would take now. The base face burns your own Claude's Datacenter too while it stands in its backrow zone; animated, it is a Unit and stays ([[R588]]). Tunes: none. |

The play that set it off resolves first ([[§10.5]] step 7); then End the turn ([[§6.3]], `turnCutShort`): the rest of that effect list resolves and the turn ends as if they had pressed End turn, every end-of-turn step running. The countered Spell goes to its owner's graveyard, treated as never played: no `cardPlayed`, no counts, no Echo repeats; its mana stays spent. ### 8.8 Meditative

Ninety-nine cards, `meditative-001` to `meditative-099`, from the designer's list of 2026-10-08 (issue #496, `JackiOh_Meditative_Cards.md`), each token a card defines right after it (index N.k, id `meditative-NNN-k`): thirty tokens, 129 rows. The designer used two numbers twice, listed one card twice and left one unnumbered; the first card listed under a number keeps it, and the others are renumbered: Shade-iris keeps #28 and its token Ancient Curse is #28.1, while Showdown becomes #98; Growing Felinor keeps #93 and its tokens #93.1 to #93.3, while Paranoia becomes #99; the unnumbered Small Time Recruits takes the hole at #62; the Empty Plot the designer numbered #100.1 is #97.1, the first of Jlockheed's Evil Blueprints' nine buildings (#97.1 to #97.9); and the Clip-Farming Lawyer listed twice, identically, is one card (#27). #98 and #99 close the table, in number order. The cards print Nerf and Buff, which players read for the engine's Degrade and Upgrade ([[§6.3]], [[R386]]): the identifiers, the events and `tune_once` keep their names, as [[R373]] kept `library`; "Buff it twice" is two Upgrades, and "+2/+2" is a plain stat buff. A token carries `rarity: "Token"` and the designer's rarity as `printedRarity`, shown in parentheses, as in [[§8.7]]. The designer gave five cards no rarity, and the rubric above gives them one: Showdown, Dragon Fruit, Small Time Recruits and Jlarna Rare, House Party Common. Meditative: 26 Common, 29 Rare, 22 Epic, 16 Legendary, 6 Mythic (99). The set is in the catalog but does not ship until the last part of its patch ([[R1420]]): its rows describe the cards being built, batch by batch (BUILD M10), from the design brief `docs/meditative-set.md`. In this section "M #N" is Meditative card N, and "#N", "C #N" and "C+ #N" are Core, Classic and Classic+ cards as elsewhere. A mechanic the engine lacks is named by its system in the brief (ME-TURN, ME-CN, ME-TRIG and the rest) and by the ruling the brief proposes for it (MD-A7 and the like), which takes an R number as the part that proves it lands. "Tunes:" is as in [[§8.6]].

The Spell resolves into a `number` prompt for the crafted card's cost, 0 to `CRAFT_MAX_COST` (4), then a new `craft` prompt, a block editor in Scratch's manner whose options are `CRAFT_PRESETS` (4) complete recipes the engine rolls within the budget from the match rng (a Unit, a Spell, a Field Spell, a Trap) and whose answer is a preset or any recipe of the player's own (MD-A30). The blocks are a card file's own pieces (type, stats, keywords, hooks and effect verbs), each priced in points (at most `CRAFT_POINTS_BASE` 2 + `CRAFT_POINTS_PER_MANA` 5 × the cost) and in lines of code (at most `CRAFT_LOC_BUDGET` 24, the card file's frame of 3 included), with at most `CRAFT_MAX_EFFECTS` 8 effects and each number 1 to `CRAFT_MAX_N` 10, every price a named constant. An invalid recipe is refused with the state unchanged and the prompt still open. A valid one is compiled into a transient definition, as Fuse's are ([[R77]]): its id names its recipe, or a digest of it ([[R179]], [[R468]]), so its script rebuilds in any process; it is of the Meditative set, not a token, Mythic, with no tags, its `loc` the counted lines, every number a param that Nerf and Buff reach ([[R386]]), and its Radiant face doubles every number and stat (`CRAFT_RADIANT_MULTIPLIER` 2) (MD-A32). It goes to your hand at its cost, Radiant on the Radiant face (Radiant being the flag, [[§5.2]]); a full hand burns it ([[§2.4]]). The AI and a timeout ([[R79]]) answer only with presets, `legal_actions` enumerating no free recipe ([[R90]]) (MD-A31). The client's editor shows the engine's own verdict through the WASM module, a points meter and a lines-of-code meter; the opponent sees a prompt open, then a card under the sentinel ([[R97]]), and its definition once a card of it is readable ([[R243]]). Distinct from #99 Craft a Card ([[R739]]). The turn clock runs while crafting, as for C+ #42 KY's Test ([[R420]]) (⚠ designer). Tunes: none; the crafter's prices are config. |

An extra turn is a whole [[§2.2]] turn: the turn count grows (max mana with it, and [[R2]]'s cap counts it), with its refresh, Brittle tick, start-of-turn triggers and draw; Units are no longer summoning sick; the gainer's "next turn" effects land on it and the opponent's wait for their next turn (MD-A18). Gained on your own turn it follows at once; gained by a cast on the opponent's turn it follows your next turn; several are taken one by one (MD-A19). | 20 | Aestheticize the Game | Mythic | 1 | Spell, Quickdraw, Wincon |  | Choose one; for the rest of the game you also win while it is true: your hero has 100 or more health; your graveyard holds 100 or more cards; your Units have 100 or more Attack and 100 or more Health in total | 90 each; then draw a card | Quickdraw ([[R640]]); the Wincon tag is new. ME-WIN's chosen condition: the three modes are declared ([[R81]]), and the chosen one installs a rest-of-game player effect on the caster, `altWin { condition, threshold }` (never expiring, [[R458]]), public on the view with its progress ("Health 47/100"). A condition lasts the rest of the game and cannot be removed; several kept conditions win on any one (MD-A21). At that point a player whose hero is at 0 or less loses even if a win of theirs holds; otherwise a player meeting a win (a condition, or M #8's Ascent 10) wins (`GameOverReason::AltWin`, `WonByEffect`) and two winners draw; a win effect takes hold at the check closing its effect list ([[R59]]) (MD-A23). A card's element is the Hetu (河图) element of its index's last digit: 1 and 6 水, 2 and 7 火, 3 and 8 木, 4 and 9 金, 5 and 0 土; an index with no digit is 土, and a fused or crafted card has its first ingredient's (MD-C5). Elements are no tags, read by no pool, filter or "shares a tag" rule (MD-C11), and are shown on every readable card while a Feng Shui is on the field. Each player's last face-up play is recorded at [[§10.5]] step 4, casts included ([[R70]]), whether or not a Feng Shui stands; Traps, Field Traps, cards set face-down (ME-ALTPLAY) and countered plays are neither recorded nor judged, so the hidden rule never reveals a hidden card, and the record ignores [[R451]]'s AI-card skip (MD-C7); its two lines are `preview`s ([[R280]]) with the glyph as their display ([[R372]]). At step 3, where Gifted Program's flag is read ([[R213]], [[R214]], [[R449]]), each Feng Shui on the field judges the play from its player's last card: positive when the last element generates the new one (木→火→土→金→水→木), negative when it overcomes it (木→土→水→火→金→木), neutral otherwise and on a first play (MD-C6). A positive play becomes Radiant and resolves so; a negative one is given Brittle 2 there ([[R385]], [[R638]]) and, once it has resolved, takes a hit of 10 from Feng Shui on that player's hero through [[§4.4]] (MD-C8); Feng Shui does not judge its own play. The base face judges both players; the Radiant face rewards only its controller and punishes only the opponent, for 20; each Feng Shui judges on its own, so punishments stack (MD-C9). The hidden numbers are config (`FENG_SHUI_DAMAGE` 10, Radiant 20; `FENG_SHUI_BRITTLE` 2), printed nowhere and never tuned (MD-C10); 20 is the brief's reading of "heavily" (⚠ designer). A public `fengShui { instanceId, player, outcome }` lets the client draw 吉 or 凶. Luck X (ME-LUCK) adds X to the Lucky of every roll with a "best" that its controller's cards make; a roll with no best is untouched ([[R32]]) (MD-C12). Tunes: luck 1 ↑ (never below 1). |

The stall is rolled once from the match rng as the Spell resolves: three different random non-token CN cards (every set, [[R380]], [[R382]], [[R60]], never this one, [[R387]]), two Auspicious Rocks (M #39.1) and one random AI generated card (one AI-tagged card of the ten C+ #78 names). Prices are in yuan, by engine config: `YUAN_PER_COST` (10) times the printed cost out of play ([[R65]]) plus `YUAN_PER_RARITY` (5) times the rarity rank (Common 1 to Mythic 5; a token by its printed rarity, an AI card as Common), doubled for a Radiant card: a Rock is 10 ¥, a (1) AI card 15 ¥, a (2) Rare CN card 30 ¥. A new prompt kind, `market`, the caster's alone ([[§10.6]]), offers each lot they can afford (none while their hand is full), on the Radiant face one barter per hand card, and Leave; each answer is one deal, the prompt reopening ([[R113]]) until Leave or until nothing more can be done; a bought card arrives on its base face at its printed cost, hidden per zone, and unspent yuan is lost (MD-C13). A barter exiles the hand card (public) and adds its price, doubled if it is Radiant, to the yuan; any number may be bartered (MD-C14). The AI answers it as any prompt, enumerating at most 6 lots, 10 barters and Leave and simulating each on determinized copies ([[R185]]); a timeout ([[R79]]) and the random policy answer uniformly ([[§10.7]]). While a card with a new judging flag acts on your field, each `play` action the opponent makes is judged on the state before it, ahead of [[§10.5]] step 1: every card they could play now is scored from their own view (the dry-run base, whose stand-ins conceal your hand, both libraries and your face-down traps, [[R222]], and a per-instance dry run at the card's own price, tuning and face), and the play is optimal when its card's score equals the top score, ties sharing it; the verdict waits for the trigger on that play's `cardResolved` ([[R17]]); a countered play was never played, and casts ([[R70]]) are no decisions and are not judged (MD-D28). Only the Cane's attack makes it public, telling you that a better-scored card was in their hand; the judge's choice is shown to no one. The Radiant face also hears emotes (NEW: ME-EMOTE, revising [[R643]]): an `Emote` action, legal for either seat when no prompt or mulligan is open and only while the other seat controls an acting card that hears emotes, with a public `emoted` event its trigger answers; the actor relays an emote that passed [[R643]]'s gate as now and also mints it as that seat's `Emote` action through the reduce queue (logged and hashed), refusing one that arrives on the action channel; hotseat and practice send it when the view says emotes are heard, the AI persona's included ([[R645]]); the random policy and My Pawn never emote; every other emote stays [[R643]]'s cosmetic relay (MD-D29).
